People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

That’s when this whole… change in me started.\n\nDoctor: Since your daughter left, have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe. I haven’t really been keeping track like that. It’s just been… heavier than usual, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Since your daughter left, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I used to mess with my tomato plants, sand wood out at my bench, watch the Falcons, stay after church talking a bit. Lately I just kind of look at all that like it belongs to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings mostly come up when you’re thinking about your daughter being away?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I do think about her, yeah. But I can’t say it’s only then. It just kind of sits there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know about nearly every day. Some days feel pretty low, but I haven’t really been counting it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend a lot of time longing for or preoccupied with your daughter since she left?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I think about her some, sure. Work’s been… not good. I made some mistakes, enough that my supervisor found me sitting in my truck before dawn and got occupational health involved.\n\nAt home, I’ve been letting bills and stuff pile up. Things I’d normally just handle, I just… haven’t.\n\nDoctor: For the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say nearly every day. I’ve felt down a lot, yeah… but I haven’t really been tracking it like that.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been waking up much earlier than usual and not able to get back to sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed that specifically. My sleep hasn’t been something I’ve kept close track of.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt sad or empty most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve had some low stretches, but saying most of the day… I don’t know. I haven’t been measuring it like that.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you noticed your appetite being lower than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has been lower. Most days I kind of have to make myself eat a few bites. Stuff I usually like just sits there, at work or at home, and I end up tossing it.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. It’s just been… heavier than usual, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Since your daughter left, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I used to mess with my tomato plants, sand wood out at my bench, watch the Falcons, stay after church talking a bit. Lately I just kind of look at all that like it belongs to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings mostly come up when you’re thinking about your daughter being away?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I do think about her, yeah. But I can’t say it’s only then. It just kind of sits there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know about nearly every day. Some days feel pretty low, but I haven’t really been counting it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend a lot of time longing for or preoccupied with your daughter since she left?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I think about her some, sure. But “a lot of time,” I’m not really sure how to answer that.\n\nDoctor: Even when you’re not thinking about your daughter, do you still feel down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’m not sure. Sometimes I feel off even when I’m not really thinking about her, but I haven’t paid close attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. Work’s been… not good. I made some mistakes, enough that my supervisor found me sitting in my truck before dawn and got occupational health involved.\n\nAt home, I’ve been letting bills and stuff pile up. Things I’d normally just handle, I just… haven’t.\n\nDoctor: For the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say nearly every day. I’ve felt down a lot, yeah… but I haven’t really been tracking it like that.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been waking up much earlier than usual and not able to get back to sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed that specifically. My sleep hasn’t been something I’ve kept close track of.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt sad or empty most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve had some low stretches, but saying most of the day… I don’t know. I haven’t been measuring it like that.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you noticed your appetite being lower than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has been lower. Most days I kind of have to make myself eat a few bites. Stuff I usually like just sits there, at work or at home, and I end up tossing it.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… I get worn out, but I don’t know if it’s more than usual. I haven’t really paid attention to it like that.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been feeling like a burden to your family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s part of it. I’ve been having thoughts like… maybe my family would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. That scared me enough that I told my supervisor and came in.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… hopeless sounds strong. I’ve had some pretty dark thoughts, but I’m not sure I’d put that word on it.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe. I keep thinking about my daughter leaving, and I end up telling myself I must’ve failed her somehow. Like if I’d been a better father, maybe I would’ve done something different.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
